The [Atlantic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Council) have described this as the practice of collective punishment, and therefore as a [war crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_war_crimes) prohibited under international humanitarian law and [Geneva convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_convention).[^cite_note-325]

In March 2014, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) reported that pro-Ukrainian activists and journalists had been attacked, abducted, and tortured by "self-defense" groups.[^cite_note-HRW140314-326] Some Crimeans were simply "disappeared" with no explanation.[^cite_note-News-327]

On 9 May 2014, the new "anti-extremist" [amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amendment) to the [Criminal Code of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Code_of_Russia), passed in December 2013, came into force. Article 280.1 designated incitement of violation of territorial integrity of the Russian Federation[^cite_note-UKRF280-1-328] (incl. calls for secession of Crimea from Russia[^cite_note-329]) as a criminal offense in Russia, punishable by a fine of 300 thousand [roubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruble) or imprisonment up to 3 years. If such statements are made in public media or the internet, the punishment could be obligatory works up to 480 hours or imprisonment up to five years.[^cite_note-UKRF280-1-328]

According to a report released on the Russian government-run President of Russia's Council on Civil Society and Human Rights website, Tatars who were opposed to Russian rule have been persecuted, Russian law restricting freedom of speech has been imposed, and the new Russian authorities "liquidated" the Kyiv Patriarchate Orthodox church on the peninsula.[^cite_note-unpoTimeline-330][^cite_note-Washington_Post-47] The Crimean Tatar television station was also shut down by the Russian authorities.[^cite_note-News-327]



Russian president Putin meeting with representatives of the [Crimean Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_Tatars), 16 May 2014

On 16 May the new Russian authorities of Crimea issued a ban on the annual commemorations of the anniversary of the [deportation of the Crimean Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deportation_of_the_Crimean_Tatars) by Stalin in 1944, citing "possibility of provocation by extremists" as a reason.[^cite_note-331] Previously, when Crimea was controlled by Ukraine, these commemorations had taken place every year. The Russian-installed Crimean authorities also banned [Mustafa Dzhemilev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Dzhemilev), a human rights activist, [Soviet dissident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_dissidents), member of the Ukrainian parliament, and former chairman of the Mejlis of the Crimean Tatars from entering Crimea.[^cite_note-ft-332] Additionally, Mejlis reported, that officers of Russia's [Federal Security Service](385990.md) (FSB) raided Tatar homes in the same week, on the pretense of "suspicion of terrorist activity".[^cite_note-reut-333] The Tatar community eventually did hold commemorative rallies in defiance of the ban.[^cite_note-ft-332][^cite_note-reut-333] In response Russian authorities flew helicopters over the rallies in an attempt to disrupt them.[^cite_note-bbc2-334]

In May 2015, a local activist, Alexander Kostenko, was sentenced to four years in a [penal colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penal_colony). His lawyer, Dmitry Sotnikov, said that the case was fabricated and that his client had been beaten and starved. Crimean prosecutor [Natalia Poklonskaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalia_Poklonskaya) accused Kostenko of making [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi) gestures during the Maidan protests, and that they were judging "not just \[Kostenko], but the very idea of [fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism) and Nazism, which are trying to raise their head once again". Sotnikov responded that "There are fabricated cases in Russia, but rarely such humiliation and physical harm. A living person is being tortured for a political idea, to be able to boast winning over fascism".[^cite_note-AFP150515-335] In June 2015, [Razom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Razom) released a report compiling human rights abuses in Crimea.[^cite_note-Razom150615-336][^cite_note-DB150623-337] In its 2016 annual report, the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) made no mention of human rights abuses in Crimea because Russia had not allowed its monitors to enter.[^cite_note-KP160621-338]

In February 2016 human rights defender [Emir-Usein Kuku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emir-Usein_Kuku) from Crimea was arrested and accused of belonging to the Islamist organization [Hizb ut-Tahrir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hizb_ut-Tahrir) although he denies any involvement in this organization. [Amnesty International](18947898.md) has called for his immediate release.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340]



Civilians from Crimea conscripted into the Russian army during the [2022 Russian mobilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russian_mobilization)

On 24 May 2014, Ervin Ibragimov, a former member of the [Bakhchysarai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakhchysarai) Town Council and a member of the World Congress of Crimean Tatars went missing. CCTV footage from a camera at a nearby shop documents that Ibragimov had been stopped by a group of men and that he is briefly speaking to the men before being forced in their van.[^cite_note-341] According to the [Kharkiv Human Rights Protection Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharkiv_Human_Rights_Protection_Group) Russian authorities refuse to investigate the disappearance of Ibragimov.[^cite_note-342]

In May 2018 [Server Mustafayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Server_Mustafayev), the founder and coordinator of the human rights movement Crimean Solidarity was imprisoned by Russian authorities and charged with "membership of a terrorist organisation". [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Front Line Defenders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_Line_Defenders) demand his immediate release.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344]

On 12 June 2018, Ukraine lodged a [memorandum](1050798.md) weighing about 90 kg, consisting of 17,500 pages of text in 29 volumes to the UN's [International Court of Justice](14918.md) about [racial discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_discrimination) by Russian authorities in occupied Crimea and state financing of terrorism by Russian Federation in [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas).[^cite_note-urlUAWire_-_Ukraine_files_memorandum_with_UN_Court_of_Justice_containing_evidence_of_Russias_involvement_in_financing_of_terrorism-345][^cite_note-urlUkraine_submits_to_ICJ_evidence_of_Russian_crimes_in_Crimea,_Donbas|_UNIAN-346]

Between 2015 and 2019 over 134,000 people living in Crimea applied for and were issued Ukrainian passports.[^cite_note-347]

### Crimean public opinion



Concert to mark the fifth anniversary of annexation in [Simferopol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simferopol), Crimea, 18 March 2019

Prior to Russian occupation, support for joining Russia was 23% in a 2013 poll, down from 33% in 2011.[^cite_note-IRI_CrimeaPoll2013-348] A joint survey by American government agency [Broadcasting Board of Governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadcasting_Board_of_Governors) and polling firm [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_\(company\)) was taken during April 2014.[^cite_note-FFS6464-349] It polled 500 residents of Crimea. The survey found that 82.8% of those polled believed that the results of the [Crimean status referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Crimean_status_referendum) reflected the views of most residents of Crimea, whereas 6.7% said that it did not. 73.9% of those polled said that they thought that the annexation would have a positive impact on their lives, whereas 5.5% said that it would not. 13.6% said that they did not know.[^cite_note-FFS6464-349]

A comprehensive poll released on 8 May 2014 by the [Pew Research Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Centre) surveyed local opinions on the annexation.[^cite_note-PewReport-350] Despite international criticism of 16 March referendum on Crimean status, 91% of those Crimeans polled thought that the vote was free and fair, and 88% said that the Ukrainian government should recognise the results.[^cite_note-PewReport-350]

In a survey completed in 2019 by a Russian company FOM 72% of surveyed Crimean residents said their lives have improved since annexation. At the same time only 39% Russians living in the mainland said the annexation was beneficial for the country as a whole which marks a significant drop from 67% in 2015.[^cite_note-351]

Whilst the Russian government actively cited local opinion polls to argue that the annexation was legitimate (i.e. supported by the population of the territory in question),[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] some authors have cautioned against using surveys concerning identities and support for the annexation conducted in "oppressive political environment" of Russian-held Crimea.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355]

## Ukrainian response

See also: [Do not buy Russian goods!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Do_not_buy_Russian_goods!), [Boycott Russian Films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott_Russian_Films), [Putin khuylo!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putin_khuylo!), and [Day of Resistance to Occupation of Crimea and Sevastopol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Day_of_Resistance_to_Occupation_of_Crimea_and_Sevastopol)



A rally in Kyiv against the Russian invasion of Crimea, 2 March 2014

Immediately after the treaty of accession was signed in March, the [Ukrainian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Ukraine\)) summoned the Provisional Principal of Russia in Ukraine to present *[note verbale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Note_verbale)* of protest against Russia's recognition of the Republic of Crimea and its subsequent annexation.[^cite_note-356] Two days later, the [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) condemned the treaty[^cite_note-357] and called Russia's actions "a gross violation of [international law](8195726.md)". The Rada called on the international community to avoid recognition of the "so-called Republic of Crimea" or the annexation of Crimea and Sevastopol by Russia as new federal subjects.

On 15 April 2014, the Verkhovna Rada declared the [Autonomous Republic of Crimea and Sevastopol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Republic_of_Crimea) to be under "provisional [occupation](816214.md)" by the Russian military[^cite_note-358] and imposed [travel restrictions](1270497.md) on Ukrainians visiting Crimea.[^cite_note-apr15-359] The territories were also deemed "inalienable parts of Ukraine" subject to Ukrainian law. Among other things, the special law approved by the Rada restricted foreign citizens' movements to and from the Crimean Peninsula and forbade certain types of entrepreneurship.[^cite_note-360] The law also forbade activity of government bodies formed in violation of Ukrainian law and designated their acts as [null and void](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Null_and_void).[^cite_note-361]



Following Russia's annexation of Crimea, Ukraine blocked the [North Crimean Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Crimean_Canal), which provided 85% of Crimea's drinking water.

[^cite_note-362]

Ukrainian authorities greatly reduced the volume of [water flowing into Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canals_in_Ukraine) via the [North Crimean Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Crimean_Canal) due to huge debt for water supplied in the previous year, threatening the viability of the peninsula's agricultural crops, which are heavily dependent on irrigation.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364]

The Ukrainian National Council for TV and Radio Broadcasting instructed all cable operators on 11 March 2014 to stop transmitting a number of Russian channels, including the international versions of the main state-controlled stations, [Russia-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia-1), [Channel One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_One_Russia) and [NTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTV_\(Russia\)), as well as news channel [Russia-24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia-24).[^cite_note-365]

In March 2014, activists began organising [flash mobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flash_mob) in supermarkets to urge customers [not to buy Russian goods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Do_not_buy_Russian_goods!) and to boycott Russian [gas stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_stations), banks, and concerts. In April 2014, some cinemas in [Kyiv](585629.md), [Lviv](49968.md), and [Odesa](55545.md) began [shunning Russian films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott_Russian_Films).[^cite_note-366]

On 2 December 2014, Ukraine created a [Ministry of Information Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_Policy_\(Ukraine\)), with one of its goals being, according to first Minister of Information, [Yuriy Stets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuriy_Stets), to counteract "Russian information aggression".[^cite_note-367]

In December 2014, Ukraine halted all train and bus services to Crimea.[^cite_note-368]

On 16 September 2015, the [Ukrainian parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_parliament) voted for the law that sets 20 February 2014 as the official date of the Russian temporary occupation of the Crimean Peninsula.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] On 7 October 2015, the [president of Ukraine](20688479.md) signed the law into force.[^cite_note-371]

The [Ministry of Temporarily Occupied Territories and IDPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Temporarily_Occupied_Territories_and_IDPs) was established by the Ukrainian government on 20 April 2016 to manage occupied parts of [Donetsk, Luhansk and Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temporarily_occupied_and_uncontrolled_territories_of_Ukraine_\(2014-present\)) regions affected by Russian military intervention of 2014.[^cite_note-NMC20416-372] By 2015, the number of [IDPs](427408.md) registered in Ukraine who had fled from Russian-occupied Crimea was 50,000.[^cite_note-373]

## Russian response

See also: [Crimean speech of Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_speech_of_Vladimir_Putin) and [2022 Moscow rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Moscow_rally)



At least 30,000 people at 15 March protests, named [March of Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Russian_anti-war_protests), which took place in Moscow a day before the Crimean referendum

In a poll published on 24 February 2014 by the state-owned [Russian Public Opinion Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Public_Opinion_Research_Center), only 15% of those Russians polled said 'yes' to the question: "Should Russia react to the overthrow of the legally elected authorities in Ukraine?"[^cite_note-374]

The [State Duma](1010187.md) Committee on [Commonwealth of Independent States](36870.md) Affairs, headed by [Leonid Slutsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Slutsky_\(politician\)), visited [Simferopol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simferopol) on 25 February 2014 and said: "If the parliament of the Crimean autonomy or its residents express the wish to join the Russian Federation, Russia will be prepared to consider this sort of application. We will be examining the situation and doing so fast".[^cite_note-Rothkopf-375] They also stated that in the event of a [referendum](51582.md) for the Crimea region joining the Russian Federation, they would consider its results "very fast".[^cite_note-gordonua.com-376] Later Slutsky announced that he was misunderstood by the Crimean press, and no decision regarding simplifying the process of acquiring Russian citizenship for people in Crimea had been made yet.[^cite_note-aif.ru-377] He also added that if "fellow Russian citizens are in jeopardy, you understand that we do not stay away".[^cite_note-378] On 25 February, in a meeting with Crimean politicians, he stated that Viktor Yanukovych was still the legitimate president of Ukraine.[^cite_note-379] That same day, the Russian Duma announced it was determining measures so that [Russians in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians_in_Ukraine) who "did not want to break from the [Russian world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_world)" could acquire Russian citizenship.[^cite_note-Интерфакс_Interfax-380]

On 27 February, the Russian governing agencies presented the new law project on granting citizenship.[^cite_note-381]

The [Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](4432213.md) called on the West and particularly [NATO](21133.md) to "abandon the provocative statements and respect the neutral status of Ukraine".[^cite_note-mid_accusations-382] In its statement, the ministry claims that the [agreement on settlement of the crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agreement_on_settlement_of_political_crisis_in_Ukraine), which was signed on 21 February and was witnessed by foreign ministries from Germany, Poland and France had to this date, not been implemented[^cite_note-mid_accusations-382] ([Vladimir Lukin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Lukin) from Russia had not signed it[^cite_note-CTV_News-383]).

On 28 February, according to [ITAR-TASS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITAR-TASS), the [Russian Ministry of Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Ministry_of_Transport) discontinued further talks with Ukraine in regards to the [Crimean Bridge](22563317.md) project.[^cite_note-384] However, on 3 March [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev), then [Prime Minister of Russia](339344.md), signed a [decree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_the_Government_of_Russia) creating a subsidiary of Russian Highways ([Avtodor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avtodor)) to build a bridge at an unspecified location along the [Kerch Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerch_Strait).[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-TV_Rain-386][^cite_note-387]

On Russian social networks, there was a movement to gather volunteers who served in the Russian army to go to Ukraine.[^cite_note-388] Many political researchers consider that after the annexation a social period in Russia coined as "[Crimean consensus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_consensus)" begun, during which the "[Rally 'round the flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rally_'round_the_flag_effect)" effect was observed in the population.[^cite_note-389]

On 28 February, President Putin stated in telephone calls with key EU leaders that it was of "extreme importance of not allowing a further escalation of violence and the necessity of a rapid normalisation of the situation in Ukraine".[^cite_note-CrimeaRussiaOccupation-390] Already on 19 February the Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs had referred to the Euromaidan revolution as the "[Brown revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_revolution)".[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392]

In Moscow, on 2 March, an estimated 27,000 rallied in support of the Russian government's decision to intervene in Ukraine.[^cite_note-csmmoscow-393] The rallies received considerable attention on Russian state TV and were officially approved by the government.[^cite_note-csmmoscow-393]

Meanwhile, on 1 March, five people who were picketing next to the Federation Council building against the invasion of Ukraine were arrested.[^cite_note-394] The next day about 200 people protested at the building of the Russian Ministry of Defence in Moscow against Russian military involvement.[^cite_note-Utro-395] About 500 people also gathered to protest on the [Manezhnaya Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manezhnaya_Square,_Moscow) in Moscow, and the same number of people on the [Saint Isaac's Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Isaac's_Square) in [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md).[^cite_note-investigator.org.ua-396] On 2 March, about eleven protesters demonstrated in [Yekaterinburg](66524.md) against Russian involvement, with some wrapped in the Ukrainian flag.[^cite_note-kp.ru-397] Protests were also held in [Chelyabinsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelyabinsk) on the same day.[^cite_note-ura.ru-398] Opposition to the military intervention was also expressed by rock musician [Andrey Makarevich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Makarevich), who wrote in particular: "You want war with Ukraine? It will not be the way [it was with Abkhazia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Georgian_war): the folks on the Maidan have been hardened and know what they are fighting for – for their country, their independence. ... We have to live with them. Still neighborly. And preferably in friendship. But it's up to them how they want to live".[^cite_note-novayagazeta.ru-399] The Professor of the Department of Philosophy at the [Moscow State Institute of International Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_State_Institute_of_International_Relations) [Andrey Zubov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Zubov) was fired for his article in *[Vedomosti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedomosti)*, criticising Russian military intervention.[^cite_note-Bershidsky-400]

On 2 March, one Moscow resident protested against Russian intervention by holding a "Stop the war" banner, but he was immediately harassed by passers-by. Police then proceeded to arrest him. A woman came forward with a fabricated charge against him, of beating up a child; however, her claim, due to lack of a victim and obviously false, was ignored by the police.[^cite_note-opposed-401] Andrei Zubov, a professor at the [Moscow State Institute of International Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_State_Institute_of_International_Relations), who compared Russian actions in Crimea to the 1938 [Annexation of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Austria) by [Nazi Germany](21212.md), was threatened. Alexander Chuyev, the leader of the pro-Kremlin [Spravedlivaya Rossiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Just_Russia_%E2%80%93_For_Truth) party, also objected to Russian intervention in Ukraine. [Boris Akunin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Akunin), a popular Russian writer, predicted that Russia's moves would lead to political and economic isolation.[^cite_note-opposed-401]



Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) (seated, middle) speaks to the press on 4 March 2014, denouncing the [Revolution of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity) as an "unconstitutional coup", and insisting that Moscow has a right to protect Russians in Ukraine.

[^cite_note-402]

President Putin's approval rating among the Russian public increased by nearly 10% since the crisis began, up to 71.6%, the highest in three years, according to a poll conducted by the [All-Russian Center for Public Opinion Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VTSIOM), released on 19 March.[^cite_note-approval-403][^cite_note-theguardian.com-404] Additionally, the same poll showed that more than 90% of Russians supported unification with the Crimean Republic.[^cite_note-approval-403] According to a 2021 study in the *[American Political Science Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Political_Science_Review)*, "three quarters of those who rallied to Putin after Russia annexed Crimea were engaging in at least some form of dissembling and that this rallying developed as a rapid cascade, with social media joining television in fueling perceptions this was socially desirable".[^cite_note-405]

On 4 March, at a press conference in [Novo-Ogaryovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novo-Ogaryovo), President Putin expressed his view on the situation that if a revolution took place in Ukraine, it would be a new country with which Russia had not concluded any treaties.[^cite_note-putin_renounced-406] He offered an analogy with the events of 1917 in Russia, when as a result of the revolution the [Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire) fell apart and a new state was created.[^cite_note-putin_renounced-406] However, he stated Ukraine would still have to honour its debts.



Around 100,000 people gathered in Crimean Sevastopol at Victory Day parade.

Russian politicians speculated that there were already 143,000 Ukrainian refugees in Russia.[^cite_note-refugees-407] The [Ukrainian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Ukraine\)) refuted those claims of refugee increases in Russia.[^cite_note-408] At a briefing on 4 March 2014, the director of the department of information policy of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Ukraine [Yevhen Perebyinis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yevhen_Perebyinis) said that Russia was misinforming its own citizens as well as the entire international community to justify its own actions in the Crimea.[^cite_note-409]

On 5 March, an anchor of the Russian-controlled TV channel [RT America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_America), [Abby Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abby_Martin_\(journalist\)), criticized her employer's biased coverage of the military invervention.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-Piers-411] Also on 5 March 2014, another RT America anchor, [Liz Wahl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liz_Wahl), of the network's Washington, DC bureau, resigned on air, explaining that she could not be "part of a network that whitewashes the actions of Putin" and citing her Hungarian ancestry and the memory of the Soviet repression of the [Hungarian Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1956) as a factor in her decision.[^cite_note-Kirchick-412]

In early March, Igor Andreyev, a 75-year-old survivor of the [Siege of Leningrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Leningrad), attended an anti-war rally against the Russian intervention in Crimea and was holding a sign that read "Peace to the World". The riot police arrested him, and a local pro-government lawyer then accused him of being a supporter of "fascism". The retiree, who lived on a 6,500-ruble monthly pension, was fined 10,000 rubles.[^cite_note-413]

Prominent dissident [Mikhail Khodorkovsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Khodorkovsky) said that Crimea should stay within Ukraine with broader autonomy.[^cite_note-Interfax-414]

[Tatarstan](67135.md), a republic within Russia populated by [Volga Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volga_Tatars), has sought to alleviate concerns about the treatment of Tatars by Russia, as Tatarstan is an oil-rich and economically successful republic in Russia.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-415] On 5 March, [President of Tatarstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Tatarstan) [Rustam Minnikhanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rustam_Minnikhanov) signed an agreement on co-operation between Tatarstan and the Aksyonov government in Crimea that implied collaboration between ten government institutions as well as significant financial aid to Crimea from Tatarstan businesses.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-415] On 11 March, Minnikhanov was in Crimea on his second visit and attended as a guest in the Crimean parliament chamber during the vote on the declaration of sovereignty pending 16 March referendum.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-415] The Tatarstan's Mufti [Kamil Samigullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamil_Samigullin) invited Crimean Tatars to study in madrasas in [Kazan](202295.md), and declared support for their "brothers in faith and blood".[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-415] [Mustafa Dzhemilev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Dzhemilev), a former leader of the [Crimean Tatar Majlis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mejlis_of_the_Crimean_Tatar_People), believed that forces that were suspected to be Russian forces should leave the Crimean Peninsula,[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-415] and asked the [UN Security Council](31956.md) to send peacekeepers into the region.[^cite_note-416]



Hotel in [Kerch](47638.md), Crimea, 2015

On 15 March, thousands of protesters (estimates varying from 3,000 by official sources up to 50,000 claimed by the opposition) in Moscow marched against Russian involvement in Ukraine, many waving Ukrainian flags.[^cite_note-417] At the same time, a pro-government (and pro-referendum) rally occurred across the street, counting in the thousands as well (officials claiming 27,000 with the opposition claiming about 10,000).

In June 2015 [Mikhail Kasyanov](254273.md) stated that all Russian Duma decisions on Crimea annexation were illegal from the international point of view and the annexation was provoked by false accusations of discrimination of Russian nationals in Ukraine.[^cite_note-418]

As of January 2019, [Arkady Rotenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arkady_Rotenberg) through his [Stroygazmontazh LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stroygazmontazh) and his companies building the [Crimean Bridge](22563317.md) along with [Nikolai Shamalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Shamalov) and [Yuri Kovalchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuri_Kovalchuk) through their [Rossiya Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rossiya_Bank) have become the most important investors in Russia's development of the annexed Crimea.[^cite_note-Insru29012019-419]

## International response

Main article: [International reactions to the annexation of Crimea by the Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reactions_to_the_annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation)

Further information: [Political status of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_status_of_Crimea) and [United Nations General Assembly Resolution 68/262](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_68/262)

See also: [Crimea Platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimea_Platform)



International reaction to the 2014 Crimean crisis according to official governmental statements.

[\[note 6\]](#cite_note-420)

&#x20; Condemnation of Russian actions as a military intervention or invasion

&#x20; Condemnation of Russian actions

&#x20; Support for Ukrainian territorial integrity

&#x20; Statements only voicing concern or hope for peaceful resolution to the conflict

&#x20; Recognition of Russian interests

&#x20; Support for Russian actions and/or condemnation of the Ukrainian interim government

---

&#x20; Ukraine

&#x20; Russia

&#x20; No official statements / No data available

There has been a range of international reactions to the annexation. In March 2014, the [UN General Assembly](31957.md) passed a non-binding resolution 100 in favour, 11 against and 58 abstentions in the 193-nation assembly that declared Crimea's Moscow-backed referendum invalid.[^cite_note-CHARBONNEAU_AND_DONATH-421] In a move supported by the Lithuanian president,[^cite_note-CNBC-422] the United States government imposed sanctions against persons they deem to have violated or assisted in the violation of Ukraine's sovereignty.[^cite_note-EO13660-423] The European Union suspended talks with Russia on economic and visa-related matters, and is considering more stringent sanctions against Russia in the near future, including asset freezes.[^cite_note-EUsanctions-424][^cite_note-mainichi.jp-425] while Japan announced sanctions which include suspension of talks relating to military, space, investment, and visa requirements.[^cite_note-426] The United Kingdom qualified the referendum vote in Crimea of being "farcical", "illegal" and "illegitimate".[^cite_note-427]

Ukraine and other countries said that Russia signed several treaties guaranteeing Ukrainian territorial integrity. These include the 1991 [Belavezha Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belavezha_Accords) that established the [Commonwealth of Independent States](36870.md), the 1975 [Helsinki Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki_Accords), the 1994 [Budapest Memorandum on Security Assurances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Memorandum_on_Security_Assurances)[^cite_note-428] and the 1997 [Treaty on friendship, cooperation and partnership between the Russian Federation and Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_Treaty_on_the_Status_and_Conditions_of_the_Black_Sea_Fleet).[^cite_note-UkraineStatement-198][^cite_note-NATOStatement-199][^cite_note-429]

The [European Commission](9974.md) decided on 11 March 2014 to enter into a full free-trade agreement with Ukraine within the year.[^cite_note-430] On 12 March, the [European Parliament](9581.md) rejected the upcoming referendum on independence in Crimea, which they saw as manipulated and contrary to international and Ukrainian law.[^cite_note-431] The [G7](1196634.md) bloc of developed nations (the [G8](59755.md) minus Russia) made a joint statement condemning Russia and announced that they would suspend preparations for [the planned G8 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/40th_G8_summit) in [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi) in June.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] NATO condemned Russia's occupation of Crimea and stated that it was a breach of international law[^cite_note-434] while the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) expressed its full support for the territorial integrity and national unity of Ukraine.[^cite_note-435] The [Visegrád Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group) has issued a joint statement urging Russia to respect Ukraine's territorial integrity and for Ukraine to take into account its minority groups to not further break fragile relations. It has urged for Russia to respect Ukrainian and international law and in line with the provisions of the [1994 Budapest Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Budapest_Memorandum).[^cite_note-Visegrad-436]

&#x20;NATO secretary general [Anders Fogh Rasmussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anders_Fogh_Rasmussen) said in a speech on 19 March 2014:

> The annexation of Crimea through a so-called referendum held at gunpoint is illegal and illegitimate. ... it undermines our security. Not just NATO's or Ukraine's security, but also Russia's. If the rules don't apply, if agreements are not honored, certainly Russia also stands to suffer the consequences. ... Russia was among those who [committed in 1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Memorandum) to respect Ukraine's territorial integrity and sovereignty. Russia pledged not to threaten or use force against Ukraine. By turning its back on that agreement, Russia has called into question its credibility and reliability ... No one wants to turn away from our cooperation with Russia. But no one can ignore that Russia has violated the very principles upon which that cooperation is built.[^cite_note-437]

On 1 April 2014, NATO foreign ministers issued a joint statement condemning "Russia's illegal and illegitimate attempt to annex Crimea" and announcing, "We have decided to suspend all practical civilian and military cooperation between NATO and Russia. Our political dialogue in the NATO-Russia Council can continue, as necessary".[^cite_note-end_coop-56][^cite_note-1aprilstatement-438]

China said "We respect the independence, sovereignty and territorial integrity of Ukraine". A spokesman restated China's belief of non-interference in the internal affairs of other nations and urged dialogue.[^cite_note-ibtbacchi1-439][^cite_note-ibtbacchi2-440]

The Indian government called for a peaceful resolution of the situation.[^cite_note-441] Both [Syria](57134800.md) and [Venezuela](32374.md) openly supported Russian military action. Syrian president [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) said that he supports Putin's efforts to "restore security and stability in the friendly country of Ukraine", while Venezuelan President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) condemned Ukraine's "ultra-nationalist" coup.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-SFGATE-443] Sri Lanka described Yanukovych's removal as unconstitutional and considered Russia's concerns in Crimea as justified.[^cite_note-444]

Polish prime minister [Donald Tusk](648724.md) called for a change in EU energy policy as Germany's dependence on Russian gas poses risks for Europe.[^cite_note-EU_2014-445]

On 13 March 2014, German Chancellor Angela Merkel warned the Russian government it risks massive damage to Russia, economically and politically, if it refuses to change course on Ukraine,[^cite_note-GuardianRussiaMerkel-446] though close economic links between Germany and Russia significantly reduce the scope for any sanctions.[^cite_note-447]

After Russia moved to formally incorporate Crimea, some worried whether it may do the same in other regions.[^cite_note-Transdniester-448] US deputy national security advisor [Tony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Blinken) said that the Russian troops massed on the eastern Ukrainian border may be preparing to enter the country's eastern regions. Russian officials stated that Russian troops would not enter other areas.[^cite_note-Transdniester-448] US Air Force Gen. [Philip M. Breedlove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_M._Breedlove), NATO's supreme allied commander in Europe, warned that the same troops were in a position to take over the separatist Russian-speaking Moldovan province of [Transnistria](376581.md).[^cite_note-Transdniester-448] [President of Moldova](1118398.md) [Nicolae Timofti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_Timofti) warned Russia with not attempting to do this to avoid damaging its international status further.[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450]

On 9 April, the [Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_Assembly_of_the_Council_of_Europe) deprived Russia of voting rights.[^cite_note-451]

On 14 August, while visiting Crimea, Vladimir Putin ruled out pushing beyond Crimea. He undertook to do everything he could to end the conflict in Ukraine, saying Russia needed to build calmly and with dignity, not by confrontation and war which isolated it from the rest of the world.[^cite_note-PutinConciliatory-452]

### United Nations resolutions



[UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) vote on a draft resolution condemning the 2014 Crimean referendum.&#x20;

&#x20; Voted in favour of the resolution

&#x20; Abstained

&#x20; Vetoed the resolution



UN General Assembly vote on the resolution condemning the 2014 Crimean referendum.&#x20;

&#x20; In favour of considering the referendum illegal

&#x20; Abstained

&#x20; Absent when the vote took place

&#x20; Against adopting the resolution

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of vetoed United Nations Security Council resolutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_vetoed_United_Nations_Security_Council_resolutions).

On 15 March 2014, a US-sponsored resolution that went to a vote in the UN [Security Council](31956.md) to reaffirm that council's commitment to Ukraine's "sovereignty, independence, unity and territorial integrity" was not approved. Though a total of 13 council members voted in favour of the resolution and China abstained, Russia vetoed the resolution.[^cite_note-453]

On 27 March 2014, the UN General Assembly approved a [resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_68/262) describing the [referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Crimean_referendum) leading to annexation of Crimea by Russia as illegal.[^cite_note-454] The draft resolution, which was titled "Territorial integrity of Ukraine", was co-sponsored by Canada, Costa Rica, Germany, Lithuania, Poland, Ukraine and the US. It affirmed the council's commitment to the "sovereignty, political independence, unity and territorial integrity of Ukraine within its internationally recognised borders". The resolution tried to underscore that 16 March referendum held in Crimea and the city of Sevastopol has no validity and cannot form the basis for any alteration of the status of the Autonomous Republic of Crimea or of the city of Sevastopol. The resolution got 100 votes in its favour, while 11 nations voted against and 58 countries abstained from the vote. The resolution was non-binding and the vote was largely symbolic.[^cite_note-455]

Since 2014, the [UN General Assembly](31957.md) has voted several times, most recently in December 2019,[^cite_note-un2019-456] to affirm Ukraine's territorial integrity, condemn the 'temporary occupation' of Crimea, and reaffirm nonrecognition of its annexation.[^cite_note-un2019-456]

### International recognition

[Afghanistan](737.md), [Cuba](5042481.md), [North Korea](21255.md), [Kyrgyzstan](170131.md), [Nicaragua](21362.md), [Sudan](27421.md), [Syria](57134800.md), and [Zimbabwe](34399.md) have recognised the result of the [2014 referendum in Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Crimean_status_referendum).[^cite_note-457]

Four [non-UN member states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition) recognised the results of the referendum: [Abkhazia](18933375.md), [South Ossetia](318381.md), [Artsakh](1000530.md) and [Transnistria](376581.md). The Transnistrian foreign minister, [Nina Shtanski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nina_Shtanski), recognized Crimea's annexation by Russia.[^cite_note-458] [Transnistria](376581.md) sent a request on 18 March 2014 [to join the Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_Russian_annexation_of_Transnistria) following the Crimean example and in compliance with the Admission Law provisions.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] The regional councils of Italy's northern regions [Lombardy](43807.md), Liguria and [Veneto](43780.md) adopted a non-binding resolution on recognizing Crimea as part of Russia, but they revoked it in 2022 after [Russia invaded Ukraine](79400268.md).[^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463]

### Sanctions

Further information: [International sanctions during the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) and [List of people and organizations sanctioned during the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_and_organizations_sanctioned_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War)



Ukrainian checkpoint at [Kalanchak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalanchak), entering Kherson Oblast from Russian-occupied Crimea

Sanctions were imposed to prevent officials and politicians from travelling to Canada, the United States, or the European Union. They were the most wide-ranging applied to Russia since the fall of the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-Katakey-464][^cite_note-Sanctions-465]

Japan announced milder sanctions than the US and EU. These include suspension of talks relating to military, space, investment, and visa requirements.[^cite_note-466]

In response to the sanctions introduced by the US and EU, the [Russian Duma](1010187.md) unanimously passed a resolution asking for all members of the Duma to be included on the sanctions list.[^cite_note-467] Head of the [Just Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Just_Russia) party [Sergei Mironov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Mironov) said he was proud of being included on the sanctions list: "It is with pride that I have found myself on the black list, this means they have noticed my stance on Crimea".[^cite_note-468] Russian companies started pulling billions of dollars out of Western banks to avoid having their asset frozen.[^cite_note-FT_freeze-469]

Three days after the lists were published, the [Russian Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Ministry_of_Russia) published a reciprocal sanctions list of US citizens, which consisted of 10 names, including [House Speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_United_States_House_of_Representatives) [John Boehner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Boehner), Senator [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain), and two advisers to President Obama. The ministry said: "We have repeatedly warned that sanctions are a double-edged instrument and would hit the United States like a boomerang".[^cite_note-CNBC-422] Several of those sanctioned responded with pride at their inclusion on the list, including John Boehner,[^cite_note-WaPo-470] John McCain,[^cite_note-WaPo-470] [Bob Menendez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Menendez),[^cite_note-471] [Dan Coats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Coats),[^cite_note-WaPo-470] [Mary Landrieu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Landrieu), and [Harry Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Reid).[^cite_note-472]

On 24 March, Russia imposed retaliatory sanctions on 13 Canadian officials including members of the [Parliament of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Canada),[^cite_note-chaseglobeandmail-473] banning them from entering Russia. Foreign Affairs Minister [John Baird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Baird_\(Canadian_politician\)), said the sanctions were "a badge of honour".[^cite_note-474] Former [Minister of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Justice_\(Canada\)) [Irwin Cotler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irwin_Cotler) also said that he considered the sanctions a badge of honour, not a mark of exclusion.[^cite_note-chaseglobeandmail-473]

"The good news is that so far, Russia has shown no inclination to use the [Northern Distribution Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Distribution_Network) as leverage in the wake of US retaliation for its troop movements in Crimea".[^cite_note-475]

Expanded Western sanctions in mid-March coursed through financial markets, hitting the business interests of some of Russia's richest people.[^cite_note-FT_share_prices-476] The Americans centred on the heart of Moscow's leadership,[^cite_note-477] though the EU's initial list shied from targeting Putin's inner circle.[^cite_note-478] As ratings agencies [Fitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fitch_Ratings) and [Standard & Poor's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_&_Poor's) downgraded Russia's credit outlook,[^cite_note-Bloomberg_downgrade-479] Russian banks warned of a sanctions-induced recession,[^cite_note-480] the country braced for capital outflows for the first three months of 2014 to reach \$70 billion,[^cite_note-481] more than the entirety of outflows for 2013,[^cite_note-482] and Russian government-bond issues plummeted by three-quarters compared with the same period the previous year.[^cite_note-483] [Novatek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novatek), Russia's second-largest gas producer, saw \$2.5bn in market value wiped out when its shares sank by nearly 10%, rendering Putin's close friend [Gennady Timchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gennady_Timchenko), who has a 23% stake in the company, \$575m poorer.[^cite_note-FT_share_prices-476] "I do hope that there is some serious diplomatic activity going on behind the scenes," said one Russian banker,[^cite_note-484] though others were more sanguine on the question of whether the sanctions would have any enduring effect,[^cite_note-Bloomberg_downgrade-479][^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486] and Russians, top and bottom, seemed defiant.[^cite_note-487] The official Russian response was mixed.[^cite_note-488]

The then [Minister of Economic Development of the Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Economic_Development_\(Russia\)) [Alexey Ulyukaev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexey_Ulyukaev) said that introduction of sectoral sanctions will lead to a serious decline of the Russian economy: economic growth of Russia will become seriously negative, the growth of volumes of investment will be even more negative, inflation will rise, and government revenues and reserves will go down.[^cite_note-489]

As well as differences between the United States and Europe as a whole as to how to respond to the Russian-backed incursion, those same differences have played out among Eastern European countries.[^cite_note-490]

A number of Russian citizens reported that they have been denied European visas after they visited Crimea after annexation.[^cite_note-491] A Russian consumer protection watchdog OZPP published a warning for Russian tourists about this risk, explaining that from the international law point of view, Crimea is an occupied territory, after which [Roskomnadzor](15773661.md) blocked the OZPP website "for threatening territorial integrity of Russian Federation".[^cite_note-492]

In response to having its voting rights revoked, Russia in June 2017 suspended its budget payments to the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), with Russian foreign minister [Sergei Lavrov](541346.md) stating payments would not resume until all rights of Russia's delegation were fully restored.[^cite_note-493] Council Secretary General [Thorbjørn Jagland](192081.md) has suggested lifting the sanctions to avoid the impact of mounting budgetary restraints. However, Council members such as Ukraine and its supporters have argued that readmitting Russia without demanding concessions in return would amount to "caving to Russian 'blackmail'".[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495]

### Mapping



Location of the [Crimea Federal District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimea_Federal_District) within Russia in 2014

- The United Nations maps Crimea as belonging to Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-496]

- [National Geographic Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Society) stated that their policy is "to portray current reality" and "Crimea, if it is formally annexed by Russia, would be shaded gray", but also further remarked that this step does not suggest recognising the legitimacy of such.

  [^cite_note-497]

  &#x20;As of April 2014, Crimea was still displayed as part of Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-hitechmaps-498]

- As of April 2014, [Google Maps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Maps) displays Crimea as a disputed territory to most viewers.

  [^cite_note-hitechmaps-498]

  &#x20;For the Russian and Ukrainian versions of the website, Crimea is marked as belonging to the corresponding country (Russia or Ukraine respectively).

  [^cite_note-hitechmaps-498]

  &#x20;Google stated that it "work(s) with sources to get the best interpretation of the border or claim lines".

  [^cite_note-499]

- [Yandex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yandex) displays Crimea as part of Russia for .ru and .com domains since the end of March 2014.

  [^cite_note-500]

  &#x20;According to the official statement, the company works with users from different countries and "displays reality that surrounds them".

  [^cite_note-501]

- As of March 2014, [Bing Maps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bing_Maps),

  [^cite_note-502]

  &#x20;[OpenStreetMap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenStreetMap) and [HERE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Here_\(company\)) displayed Crimea as belonging to Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-hitechmaps-498]

  &#x20;In particular, OpenStreetMap requested its users to refrain from editing borders and administrative relations of subdivisions located in the Autonomous Republic of Crimea and Sevastopol until 31 May 2014.

  [^cite_note-hitechmaps-498]

  &#x20;On 5 June 2014, OpenStreetMap switched to a territorial dispute option, displaying Crimea as a disputed territory belonging to both countries.

  [^cite_note-503]

- In 2015, on the [PepsiCo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PepsiCo) website, a Russian-language map was visible for a few days that depicted Crimea as a part of Russia.

  [^cite_note-504]

- The 2016 edition of a French atlas published by [Larousse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ditions_Larousse) shows Crimea as part of Russian territory: [Oleh Shamshur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Shamshur), Ukrainian Ambassador to France, expressed shock.

  [^cite_note-505]

  &#x20;Shortly after, Larousse changed the map to reflect Crimea as part of Ukraine on the Atlas on their internet version.

  [^cite_note-506]

  [^cite_note-507]

- The Italian-language magazine of [geopolitics](163225.md) *[Limes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limes_\(Italian_magazine\))* maps Crimea as a part of Russia since December 2015.

  [^cite_note-508]

  &#x20;Following protests from the Ukrainian embassy in Italy, the magazine editor Lucio Caracciolo wrote that "the map reflects reality. When Crimea and Sevastopol will be back under effective Ukrainian sovereignty, we will produce a map that reflects such reality".

  [^cite_note-509]

- The Russian version of Apple's [App Store](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/App_Store) began to show Crimea as part of Russia on 27 November 2019.

  [^cite_note-510]

## Analysis

Researchers consider the annexation of Crimea to be a [coup](25914154.md), because the Russian military seized Crimea's parliament and government buildings and instigated the replacement of its government with Russian proxies.[^cite_note-26436575Putin2-106][^cite_note-idUSBREA4M05420140524-107][^cite_note-PopovaEntangled-108][^cite_note-WilsonCrisis-109][^cite_note-MaidanWar-110] In particular, political scientist Olga Burlyuk defines Crimea events as "a coup d'état of a regional scale".[^cite_note-511]

Crimea's annexation has been described as an instance of [modern Russian imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_neo-imperialism).[^cite_note-512] It was the first time since [World War II](32927.md) that a European country had annexed part of another.[^cite_note-513] Some analysts see the annexation as the beginning of a [Second Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Cold_War) between Russia and the West.[^cite_note-514][^cite_note-D'Anieri-2023-40]

[^cite_note-11]: &#x20;There are "some contradictions and inherent problems" regarding the date on which the Russian operation began.<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-Disunited-1)</sup> Russia first deployed troops in Crimea on 26 February, seizing roads between their base in Sevastopol and the capital of Simferopol. The Ukrainian Government maintains, and the [European Court of Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights) agrees, that Russia *controlled* Crimea from 27 February 2014,<sup>[\[2\]](#cite_note-2)</sup> when unmarked Russian special forces took control of its political institutions.<sup>[\[3\]](#cite_note-3)</sup> The Russian Government later made 27 February "Special Operations Forces Day".<sup>[\[4\]](#cite_note-4)</sup> A Ukrainian law adopted by [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) on 15 April 2014 designated 19 February 2014 as the "beginning of Russian occupation in separate Ukrainian territories", as on that date Russian forces established control over the Schmidt Gas Deposit located on the Ukrainian [continental shelf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_shelf) in the [Black Sea](3386.md) not far from Crimea.<sup>[\[5\]](#cite_note-5)</sup> In 2015, the [Ukrainian parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_parliament) officially designated 20 February 2014 as "the beginning of the [temporary occupation of Crimea and Sevastopol by Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temporarily_occupied_and_uncontrolled_territories_of_Ukraine)",<sup>[\[6\]](#cite_note-6)</sup> citing the date inscribed on the Russian [medal "For the Return of Crimea"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_%22For_the_Return_of_Crimea%22).<sup>[\[7\]](#cite_note-7)</sup> In 2018, Russian Foreign Minister Sergey Lavrov claimed that the earlier "start date" on the medal was due to a "technical misunderstanding".<sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-8)</sup> President Putin stated in a [film about the annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimea._The_Way_Home) that he ordered the operation to "restore" Crimea to Russia following an all-night emergency meeting on 22–23 February 2014.<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-Disunited-1)</sup><sup>[\[9\]](#cite_note-auto-9)</sup><sup>[\[10\]](#cite_note-auto2-10)</sup>
